\\\"首次系统研究 LLM 的 self-consuming training loop\\\"\\n      - \\\"full synthetic cycle（完全替换）导致单点 collapse；data augmentation cycle 导致 diversity 缓慢下降\\\"\\n      - \\\"expanding data cycle（累积）在 50 代内保持 diversity\\\"\\n    relevance_to_us: \\\"其不同 data cycle 策略的对比直接对应我们的 alpha（synthetic ratio）实验设置\\\"\\n    metrics_studied: [\\\"diversity\\\", \\\"quality\\\"]\\n\\n  - id: \\\"truong2025entropy\\\"\\n    title: \\\"Entropy Collapse: A Universal Failure Mode of Intelligent Systems\\\"\\n    authors: \\\"Truong Xuan Khanh, Truong Quynh Hoa\\\"\\n    venue: \\\"arXiv (2512.12381)\\\"\\n    year: 2025\\n    url: \\\"https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.12381\\\"\\n    category: \\\"core-collapse\\\"\\n    role: \\\"theoretical-foundation\\\"\\n    key_findings:\\n      - \\\"证明 entropy collapse 是一阶（不连续）相变，临界点 α_c(β) = 1/(1−β)，entropy 在临界点跳变 0.698 nats\\\"\\n      - \\\"两个 universality class：Class 1（凸反馈，如 power-law）不可逆 ν=1；Class 2（线性反馈）可逆 ν=1/2\\\"\\n      - \\\"因为是一阶相变，autocorrelation/variance 等传统 early-warning signal 在 collapse 前保持有限——无预警\\\"\\n    relevance_to_us: \\\"定义了 canary 失效的 regime boundary：Class 1 凸反馈系统中传统 canary 理论上无效。我们需证明我们的 canary 在 Class 2+ regime 或非纯反馈场景中有效\\\"\\n    gaps: \\\"理论框架高度抽象，未给出 LLM 具体场景下的 α/β 估计方法；未测试哪些 canary 在接近一阶相变时仍有部分预警能力\\\"\\n    metrics_studied: [\\\"entropy\\\", \\\"autocorrelation\\\", \\\"variance\\\"]\\n\\n  - id: \\\"wang2026contaminated\\\"\\n    title: \\\"Can Generative Artificial Intelligence Survive Data Contamination? Theoretical Guarantees under Contaminated Recursive Training\\\"\\n    authors: \\\"Kevin Wang, Hongqian Niu, Didong Li\\\"\\n    venue: \\\"arXiv (2602.16065)\\\"\\n    year: 2026\\n    url: \\\"https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.16065\\\"\\n    category: \\\"core-collapse\\\"\\n    role: \\\"theoretical-foundation\\\"\\n    key_findings:\\n      - \\\"首个不限数据分布假设的正面理论结果：universal approximator 下 contaminated recursive training 仍可收敛\\\"\\n      - \\\"收敛速率 = min(baseline 模型收敛速率, 每轮真实数据比例)——真实数据比例是瓶颈\\\"\\n      - \\\"突破了先前仅限 discrete/Gaussian 分布的理论框架，适用于一般连续分布\\\"\\n    relevance_to_us: \\\"为 post-MVP α-sweep 实验提供理论上界：收敛速率由 real data fraction 决定，可预测不同 α 下的 collapse 速率\\\"\\n    gaps: \\\"universal approximator 假设在实际有限容量模型中不严格成立；未讨论 canary 指标行为\\\"\\n    metrics_studied: [\\\"convergence rate\\\", \\\"population risk\\\"]\\n\\n  - id: \\\"du2026geometric\\\"\\n    title: \\\"Escaping Mode Collapse in LLM Generation via Geometric Regulation\\\"\\n    authors: \\\"Du, Tanaka-Ishii\\\"\\n    venue: \\\"arXiv (2605.00435)\\\"\\n    year: 2026\\n    url: \\\"https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.00435\\\"\\n    category: \\\"core-collapse\\\"\\n    role: \\\"background\\\"\\n    key_findings:\\n      - \\\"将 mode collapse 重新解释为表示空间中的 geometric collapse：生成时模型内部轨迹被限制在低维子空间\\\"\\n      - \\\"提出 RMR（Reinforced Mode Regulation），在 value cache 中抑制 dominant self-reinforcing directions\\\"\\n    relevance_to_us: \\\"针对推理时 mode collapse，非迭代训练场景。但 'geometric collapse = 低维子空间坍缩' 与我们的 effective rank 指标在概念上有连接，可在 related work 中引用以说明 representation-space collapse 检测的跨场景通用性\\\"\\n    metrics_studied: [\\\"effective rank proxy\\\", \\\"representation dimensionality\\\"]\\n\\n  - id: \\\"gauthier2026alignment\\\"\\n    title: \\\"Explaining and Preventing Alignment Collapse in Iterative RLHF\\\"\\n    authors: \\\"Gauthier, Bach, Jordan\\\"\\n    venue: \\\"arXiv (2605.04266)\\\"\\n    year: 2026\\n    url: \\\"https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.04266\\\"\\n    category: \\\"core-collapse\\\"\\n    role: \\\"complementary\\\"\\n    key_findings:\\n      - \\\"迭代 RLHF 中 policy 生成 reward model 的训练数据形成反馈环路，导致 alignment collapse（policy 系统性利用 reward model 盲区）\\\"\\n      - \\\"用 Stackelberg game 建模迭代 RLHF 的环路动力学，提出 FPO（foresighted policy optimization）\\\"\\n    relevance_to_us: \\\"RLHF iterative feedback loop 与本项目 iterative synthetic data loop 结构类似——模型输出成为下一轮输入的反馈环路均导致系统性退化。其 'collapse = 反馈环路中的系统性退化' 叙事支持我们的大框架；Stackelberg game 视角可能启发 canary 的博弈论解释\\\"\\n    metrics_studied: [\\\"alignment score\\\", \\\"reward model accuracy\\\"]\\n\\n  - id: \\\"shi2026pruning\\\"\\n    title: \\\"Understanding Performance Collapse in Layer-Pruned LLMs via Decision Representation Transitions\\\"\\n    authors: \\\"Shi et al.\\\"\\n    venue: \\\"arXiv (2605.07271)\\\"\\n    year: 2026\\n    url: \\\"https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.07271\\\"\\n    category: \\\"core-collapse\\\"\\n    role: \\\"background\\\"\\n    key_findings:\\n      - \\\"层剪枝导致的 performance collapse，定义 Silent Phase（模型预测被单一选项主导 = distributional collapse）和 Decisive Phase\\\"\\n      - \\\"剪枝 Silent Phase 立即触发 collapse\\\"\\n    relevance_to_us: \\\"架构剪枝 collapse，非数据驱动。其 'distributional collapse = 预测分布退化为单峰' 的操作定义与我们 token entropy 下降检测的是同一现象的不同表现\\\"\\n    metrics_studied: [\\\"prediction distribution entropy\\\", \\\"task accuracy\\\"]\\n\\n  - id: \\\"tubingen2026diffusion\\\"\\n    title: \\\"Layer Collapse in Diffusion Language Models\\\"\\n    authors: \\\"Tübingen AI Center et al.\\\"\\n    venue: \\\"arXiv (2605.06366)\\\"\\n    year: 2026\\n    url: \\\"https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.06366\\\"\\n    category: \\\"core-collapse\\\"\\n    role: \\\"background\\\"\\n    key_findings:\\n      - \\\"LLaDA-8B 中发现层级 activation collapse：少数早期层的 activation 被单个 super-outlier 主导\\\"\\n      - \\\"剪掉此 outlier 导致模型输出退化为重复随机 token\\\"\\n    relevance_to_us: \\\"Diffusion LM 的架构 collapse，与迭代合成数据训练无关。但 activation collapse 的表征与 spectral collapse 有交集\\\"\\n    metrics_studied: [\\\"activation magnitude\\\", \\\"output repetition\\\"]\\n\\n  # ============================================================\\n  # CANARY / EARLY WARNING METRICS\\n  # ============================================================\\n  - id: \\\"chen2025scales\\\"\\n    title: \\\"Characterizing Model Behavior Under Synthetic Data Training: An Empirical Study Across Scales and Mixing Ratios\\\"\\n    authors: \\\"Anonymous\\\"\\n    venue: \\\"arXiv (2510.05133)\\\"\\n    year: 2025\\n    url: \\\"https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.05133\\\"\\n    category: \\\"canary-metrics\\\"\\n    role: \\\"direct-competitor\\\"\\n    key_findings:\\n      - \\\"**Calibration degradation precedes accuracy loss** — ECE 是 collapse 的 early warning signal\\\"\\n      - \\\"20% synthetic data 以下性能稳定，30% 以上退化加速\\\"\\n      - \\\"大模型（6.9B-12B）比小模型（410M-1.4B）更鲁棒\\\"\\n      - \\\"推理任务比检索任务更易退化\\\"\\n      - \\\"iteration 1 + 30% synthetic 时 ECE 从 0.08 升至 0.14（+75%）\\\"\\n    relevance_to_us: \\\"**最直接的竞争者/先驱**。证实 ECE 可作为 canary，但未做 lead-lag 分析，未建立多指标 panel 框架\\\"\\n    metrics_studied: [\\\"ECE\\\", \\\"accuracy\\\", \\\"perplexity\\\"]\\n\\n  - id: \\\"gambetta2024surplexity\\\"\\n    title: \\\"Learning by Surprise: Surplexity for Mitigating Model Collapse in Generative AI\\\"\\n    authors: \\\"Daniele Gambetta et al.\\\"\\n    venue: \\\"arXiv (2410.12341)\\\"\\n    year: 2024\\n    url: \\\"https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.12341\\\"\\n    category: \\\"canary-metrics\\\"\\n    role: \\\"direct-competitor\\\"\\n    key_findings:\\n      - \\\"提出 surplexity（model surprise 度量）同时用于检测 collapse 和过滤训练数据\\\"\\n      - \\\"用 semantic networks 分析文本重复性和语义多样性\\\"\\n      - \\\"用 next-token probability 定量衡量 diversity loss\\\"\\n      - \\\"高 surplexity 过滤策略至少与 human-data baseline 同效，且更能降低分布偏斜\\\"\\n      - \\\"不需要区分 human vs AI-generated data\\\"\\n    relevance_to_us: \\\"surplexity 可作为我们 canary 指标候选之一；其 semantic network 方法提供了 diversity 度量的另一视角\\\"\\n    metrics_studied: [\\\"surplexity\\\", \\\"semantic network metrics\\\", \\\"next-token probability\\\"]\\n\\n  - id: \\\"qiu2025spectral\\\"\\n    title: \\\"Spectral Alignment as Predictor of Loss Explosion in Neural Network Training\\\"\\n    authors: \\\"Haiquan Qiu, You Wu, Yingjie Tan, Yaqing Wang, Quanming Yao\\\"\\n    venue: \\\"arXiv (2510.04202)\\\"\\n    year: 2025\\n    url: \\\"https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.04202\\\"\\n    category: \\\"canary-metrics\\\"\\n    role: \\\"method-component\\\"\\n    key_findings:\\n      - \\\"Spectral Alignment (SA) 监控层输入与权重矩阵主奇异向量的分布对齐\\\"\\n      - \\\"SA sign diversity collapse 是 representational collapse 和训练发散的早期预测器\\\"\\n      - \\\"比 weight norm、gradient norm 等传统指标更早、更清晰地检测到训练失败\\\"\\n      - \\\"健康层的 SA 应有正负均衡分布，collapse 到单侧则预示不稳定\\\"\\n    relevance_to_us: \\\"SA 是潜在的高价值 canary 指标——专门设计为 early warning，可直接纳入我们的指标 panel\\\"\\n    metrics_studied: [\\\"spectral alignment\\\", \\\"weight norm\\\", \\\"gradient norm\\\"]\\n\\n  - id: \\\"he2025closer\\\"\\n    title: \\\"A Closer Look at Model Collapse: From a Generalization-to-Memorization Perspective\\\"\\n    authors: \\\"Shiliang He et al.\\\"\\n    venue: \\\"NeurIPS 2025 (Spotlight)\\\"\\n    year: 2025\\n    url: \\\"https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.16499\\\"\\n    category: \\\"canary-metrics\\\"\\n    role: \\\"direct-competitor\\\"\\n    key_findings:\\n      - \\\"识别 diffusion model collapse 中的 generalization → memorization 转变\\\"\\n      - \\\"**训练数据的 entropy 递减是 collapse 的直接驱动因素和清晰指标**\\\"\\n      - \\\"提出基于 entropy 的数据选择策略，有效缓解 collapse\\\"\\n    relevance_to_us: \\\"data entropy 作为 canary 的有效性已被验证（diffusion domain）；需测试是否在 LLM 场景同样有效\\\"\\n    metrics_studied: [\\\"entropy\\\", \\\"FID\\\", \\\"diversity\\\"]\\n\\n  - id: \\\"ba2024fill\\\"\\n    title: \\\"Fill In The Gaps: Model Calibration and Generalization with Synthetic Data\\\"\\n    authors: \\\"Yang Ba, Michelle V Mancenido, Rong Pan\\\"\\n    venue: \\\"EMNLP 2024\\\"\\n    year: 2024\\n    url: \\\"https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.10864\\\"\\n    category: \\\"canary-metrics\\\"\\n    role: \\\"background\\\"\\n    key_findings:\\n      - \\\"用 LLM 生成合成数据来修复 calibration 错误的 reliability diagram bins\\\"\\n      - \\\"推导了基于 PAC 框架的 ECE bound\\\"\\n      - \\\"合成数据可改善 calibration 但需精心设计生成策略\\\"\\n    relevance_to_us: \\\"ECE 的理论 bound 可为我们的 canary 阈值设定提供理论依据\\\"\\n    metrics_studied: [\\\"ECE\\\", \\\"accuracy\\\"]\\n\\n  - id: \\\"sigma2026spectral\\\"\\n    title: \\\"SIGMA: Scalable Spectral Insights for LLM Model Collapse\\\"\\n    authors: \\\"Yi Gu, Lingyou Pang, Xiangkun Ye, Tianyu Wang, Jianyu Lin, Carey E. Priebe, Alexander Aue\\\"\\n    venue: \\\"arXiv (2601.03385)\\\"\\n    year: 2026\\n    url: \\\"https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.03385\\\"\\n    category: \\\"canary-metrics\\\"\\n    role: \\\"direct-competitor\\\"\\n    key_findings:\\n      - \\\"基于 embedding Gram matrix 谱分析的 collapse 监控框架：Track I（确定性谱界）保守但鲁棒，Track II（随机谱缩放）灵敏但需采样\\\"\\n      - \\\"Track I/II 的分歧本身是 early-stage collapse 的诊断信号，早于表面重复性伪影\\\"\\n      - \\\"随机谱界使部分观测的 Gram matrix 也可用于大规模模型的可扩展监控\\\"\\n    relevance_to_us: \\\"最直接的 spectral canary 竞争者。我们的 effective rank 是其简化版（单一标量 vs 完整谱界）；需在 related work 中明确差异：SIGMA 提供双轨谱界，我们用单一 effective rank 换取更低计算开销和多指标 panel 集成\\\"\\n    gaps: \\\"未做 lead-lag 分析，未与 ECE/diversity 等非谱指标对比；Track I/II 的计算开销在 iterative loop 多轮场景下可能不可忽略\\\"\\n    metrics_studied: [\\\"Gram matrix eigenspectrum\\\", \\\"spectral norm bounds\\\"]\\n\\n  - id: \\\"kalinowski2026topology\\\"\\n    title: \\\"Monitoring Neural Training with Topology: A Footprint-Predictable Collapse Index\\\"\\n    authors: \\\"Alexander Kalinowski\\\"\\n    venue: \\\"arXiv (2604.26984)\\\"\\n    year: 2026\\n    url: \\\"https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.26984\\\"\\n    category: \\\"canary-metrics\\\"\\n    role: \\\"background\\\"\\n    key_findings:\\n      - \\\"提出基于 Modular Morse Homology Maintenance (MMHM) 的在线拓扑监控方案，增量更新避免每轮重建\\\"\\n      - \\\"Collapse Index (CI) 组合多尺度拓扑特征，在 LLM fine-tuning 和 temporal KGE 训练中提供低延迟 early-warning\\\"\\n      - \\\"拓扑方法捕捉表示空间的各向异性和多尺度结构丧失\\\"\\n    relevance_to_us: \\\"topology-based monitoring 是与 spectral 方法正交的 collapse 检测路线。我们排除了该方法（计算开销高、可解释性弱），但 related work 需要对比说明选择 spectral/calibration panel 而非 topology 的理由\\\"\\n    gaps: \\\"计算开销显著高于 effective rank 等标量指标；未在 iterative synthetic training loop 场景下测试；与 ECE 等轻量 canary 无对比\\\"\\n    metrics_studied: [\\\"Collapse Index\\\", \\\"persistent homology features\\\"]\\n\\n  - id: \\\"bergsma2026scaling\\\"\\n    title: \\\"Scaling with Collapse: Efficient and Predictable Training of LLM Families\\\"\\n    authors: \\\"Bergsma et al.\\\"\\n    venue: \\\"arXiv (2509.25087, rev. Mar 2026)\\\"\\n    year: 2026\\n    url: \\\"https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.25087\\\"\\n    category: \\\"canary-metrics\\\"\\n    role: \\\"background\\\"\\n    key_findings:\\n      - \\\"'Collapse' 此处指 loss curve 归一化后坍缩到通用轨迹（非 model collapse）\\\"\\n      - \\\"deviation-from-collapse 作为训练病理的早期诊断信号\\\"\\n    relevance_to_us: \\\"不同语境下的 'collapse'。但其 '偏离通用轨迹 = 训练出问题' 的监控思路与我们的 canary 理念相似——均从时序异常检测角度切入\\\"\\n    metrics_studied: [\\\"loss curve deviation\\\", \\\"scaling law exponents\\\"]\\n\\n  # ============================================================\\n  # DIVERSITY METRICS (BACKGROUND)\\n  # ============================================================\\n  - id: \\\"zhu2018texygen\\\"\\n    title: \\\"Texygen: A Benchmarking Platform for Text Generation Models\\\"\\n    authors: \\\"Yaoming Zhu et al.\\\"\\n    venue: \\\"SIGIR 2018\\\"\\n    year: 2018\\n    url: \\\"https://arxiv.org/abs/1802.01886\\\"\\n    category: \\\"canary-metrics\\\"\\n    role: \\\"background\\\"\\n    key_findings:\\n      - \\\"提出 Self-BLEU 度量：计算生成句子间的 BLEU 相似度，Self-BLEU 越高多样性越低\\\"\\n      - \\\"成为文本生成多样性评估的标准基准之一\\\"\\n    relevance_to_us: \\\"Self-BLEU 是我们 diversity canary 的经典候选\\\"\\n    metrics_studied: [\\\"Self-BLEU\\\", \\\"BLEU\\\"]\\n\\n  - id: \\\"li2016diversity\\\"\\n    title: \\\"A Diversity-Promoting Objective Function for Neural Conversation Models\\\"\\n    authors: \\\"Jiwei Li et al.\\\"\\n    venue: \\\"NAACL 2016\\\"\\n    year: 2016\\n    url: \\\"https://arxiv.org/abs/1510.03055\\\"\\n    category: \\\"canary-metrics\\\"\\n    role: \\\"background\\\"\\n    key_findings:\\n      - \\\"提出 distinct-1 / distinct-2 指标衡量生成文本的 n-gram 多样性\\\"\\n      - \\\"计算 unique n-gram 数占总 n-gram 数的比例\\\"\\n    relevance_to_us: \\\"distinct-n 是最廉价的 diversity canary 之一，计算开销极低\\\"\\n    metrics_studied: [\\\"distinct-1\\\", \\\"distinct-2\\\"]\\n\\n  # ============================================================\\n  # REPRESENTATION / SPECTRAL COLLAPSE\\n  # ============================================================\\n  - id: \\\"jing2022understanding\\\"\\n    title: \\\"Understanding Dimensional Collapse in Contrastive Self-supervised Learning\\\"\\n    authors: \\\"Li Jing et al.\\\"\\n    venue: \\\"ICLR 2022\\\"\\n    year: 2022\\n    url: \\\"https://openreview.net/forum?id=YevsQ05DEN7\\\"\\n    category: \\\"canary-metrics\\\"\\n    role: \\\"method-component\\\"\\n    key_findings:\\n      - \\\"定义 dimensional collapse：embedding 向量只跨越低维子空间\\\"\\n      - \\\"effective rank（基于归一化特征值 entropy 的指数）作为 collapse 度量\\\"\\n      - \\\"strong augmentation 和 implicit regularization 驱动低秩解\\\"\\n    relevance_to_us: \\\"effective rank 的定义和计算方式可直接迁移到我们的 embedding 空间监控\\\"\\n    metrics_studied: [\\\"effective rank\\\", \\\"singular value spectrum\\\"]\\n\\n  - id: \\\"noci2024mind\\\"\\n    title: \\\"Mind the Gap: A Spectral Analysis of Rank Collapse and Signal Propagation in Transformers\\\"\\n    authors: \\\"Lorenzo Noci et al.\\\"\\n    venue: \\\"NeurIPS 2024\\\"\\n    year: 2024\\n    url: \\\"https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.07799\\\"\\n    category: \\\"canary-metrics\\\"\\n    role: \\\"method-component\\\"\\n    key_findings:\\n      - \\\"用 Random Matrix Theory 分析 attention 层的 rank collapse\\\"\\n      - \\\"发现 attention matrix 最大两个奇异值之间的 spectral gap 是 width-collapse 的根因\\\"\\n      - \\\"rank collapse in depth: 所有 token 沿层收敛到单一表示\\\"\\n    relevance_to_us: \\\"spectral gap 作为另一种 spectral canary 候选；其 RMT 分析框架可启发我们的理论工作\\\"\\n    metrics_studied: [\\\"spectral gap\\\", \\\"singular values\\\", \\\"effective rank\\\"]\\n\\n  # ============================================================\\n  # DATA MIXING & ACCUMULATION\\n  # ============================================================\\n  - id: \\\"kazdan2025collapse\\\"\\n    title: \\\"Collapse or Thrive? Perils and Promises of Synthetic Data in a Self-Generating World\\\"\\n    authors: \\\"Joshua Kazdan, Rylan Schaeffer, Apratim Dey, Matthias Gerstgrasser, Rafael Rafailov, David L. Donoho, Sanmi Koyejo\\\"\\n    venue: \\\"ICML 2025\\\"\\n    year: 2025\\n    url: \\\"https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.16713\\\"\\n    category: \\\"data-mixing\\\"\\n    role: \\\"foundation\\\"\\n    key_findings:\\n      - \\\"系统对比 replace vs accumulate 场景\\\"\\n      - \\\"replace → collapse；accumulate → 可避免 collapse\\\"\\n      - \\\"发现 real data 和 synthetic data 之间存在非平凡的交互效应\\\"\\n      - \\\"synthetic data 的价值取决于 real data 的绝对数量\\\"\\n    relevance_to_us: \\\"直接对应我们的 alpha（mixing ratio）实验维度；其 replace vs accumulate 框架是我们实验设计的重要参考\\\"\\n    metrics_studied: [\\\"test loss\\\"]\\n\\n  - id: \\\"dey2024breaking\\\"\\n    title: \\\"Is Model Collapse Inevitable? Breaking the Curse of Recursion by Accumulating Real and Synthetic Data\\\"\\n    authors: \\\"Apratim Dey, David Donoho, et al.\\\"\\n    venue: \\\"ICML 2024\\\"\\n    year: 2024\\n    url: \\\"https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.01413\\\"\\n    category: \\\"data-mixing\\\"\\n    role: \\\"foundation\\\"\\n    key_findings:\\n      - \\\"替换数据 → 发散 population risk；累积数据 → 避免发散\\\"\\n      - \\\"证明累积策略在多种模型（GMM, HMM, LLM）上有效\\\"\\n    relevance_to_us: \\\"为我们的 alpha 实验提供理论预测：累积 vs 替换是 collapse 速率的关键调节因子\\\"\\n    metrics_studied: [\\\"population risk\\\"]\\n\\n  - id: \\\"seddik2024howbad\\\"\\n    title: \\\"How Bad is Training on Synthetic Data? A Statistical Analysis of Language Model Collapse\\\"\\n    authors: \\\"Seddik, Mohamed El Amine and Chen, Suei-Wen and Hayou, Soufiane and Youssef, Pierre and Debbah, Merouane\\\"\\n    venue: \\\"arXiv (2404.05090)\\\"\\n    year: 2024\\n    url: \\\"https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.05090\\\"\\n    category: \\\"data-mixing\\\"\\n    role: \\\"background\\\"\\n    key_findings:\\n      - \\\"纯合成数据训练不可避免 collapse\\\"\\n      - \\\"给出 synthetic data 比例上界的估计——低于此比例可避免 collapse\\\"\\n      - \\\"递归训练使原始分布尾部消失\\\"\\n    relevance_to_us: \\\"其 mixing ratio 上界估计可对比我们实验中观测到的 canary 触发阈值\\\"\\n    metrics_studied: [\\\"perplexity\\\"]\\n\\n  - id: \\\"fu2025selfconsuming\\\"\\n    title: \\\"A Theoretical Perspective: How to Prevent Model Collapse in Self-consuming Training Loops\\\"\\n    authors: \\\"Shi Fu, Yingjie Wang, Yuzhu Chen, Xinmei Tian, Dacheng Tao\\\"\\n    venue: \\\"ICLR 2025\\\"\\n    year: 2025\\n    url: \\\"https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.18865\\\"\\n    category: \\\"data-mixing\\\"\\n    role: \\\"theoretical-foundation\\\"\\n    key_findings:\\n      - \\\"最优 real data 权重在 Gaussian estimation、GLM、非参数估计中均收敛到黄金比例倒数 1/φ ≈ 0.618\\\"\\n      - \\\"最优权重 w*(λ) 作为正则化参数的函数既非单调也非凸——risk landscape 在 mixing ratio 空间上本身不是单调的\\\"\\n      - \\\"提供了 mixing ratio 非单调性的首个理论框架\\\"\\n    relevance_to_us: \\\"我们在 α-sweep 中观测到的非单调 dose-response（α=0.25 比 α=0.50 collapse 更严重）可视为该理论在 diversity metrics 上的实证验证。论文 Discussion 的理论锚点。\\\"\\n    gaps: \\\"理论基于线性回归和高斯分布，未直接推广到 LLM diversity metrics\\\"\\n    metrics_studied: [\\\"population risk\\\", \\\"optimal weight\\\"]\\n\\n  - id: \\\"boettiger2012limits\\\"\\n    title: \\\"Quantifying Limits to Detection of Early Warning for Critical Transitions\\\"\\n    authors: \\\"Carl Boettiger, Alan Hastings\\\"\\n    venue: \\\"Journal of the Royal Society Interface\\\"\\n    year: 2012\\n    doi: \\\"10.1098/rsif.2012.0125\\\"\\n    category: \\\"early-warning-signals\\\"\\n    role: \\\"methodological-precedent\\\"\\n    key_findings:\\n      - \\\"用 likelihood ratio 框架量化 early warning signals 的统计检测力——在有限长度时间序列下，EWS 检测力极低\\\"\\n      - \\\"即使系统确实在接近 tipping point，短时间序列中 autocorrelation 增加等 EWS 的统计显著性往往不足以拒绝零假设\\\"\\n      - \\\"检测力取决于时间序列长度、噪声水平、接近临界点的速率——现实数据常处于低检测力 regime\\\"\\n    relevance_to_us: \\\"与 Claim 3（方法选择敏感性）和 plan_009（TY power analysis, T=10 power=0.05-0.20）高度对齐。Boettiger & Hastings 从生态学角度量化了 EWS 在短时间序列下的统计极限，我们在 model collapse 迭代训练语境下独立发现了同样的问题——少数迭代轮次下 canary 检测力不足。他们的 power analysis 框架为我们的统计检测力论证提供了跨学科先例\\\"\\n    one_line_finding: \\\"Early warning signals 在短时间序列下统计检测力极低，量化了 EWS 检测的基本统计极限\\\"\\n    metrics_studied: [\\\"autocorrelation\\\", \\\"variance\\\", \\\"likelihood ratio\\\", \\\"statistical power\\\"]\\n\\ngaps:\\n  - \\\"无人系统做过 canary 指标的 lead-lag / Granger causality 分析——现有工作只在事后观察到指标变化，未量化哪些指标能提前多少步预测 collapse\\\"\\n  - \\\"ECE 在 collapse 场景下几乎未被系统研究——chen2025scales 是唯一报告 ECE 先于 accuracy 退化的工作，但未做因果分析\\\"\\n  - \\\"embedding effective rank 未在 iterative synthetic training 场景下被测试——仅在 contrastive learning/transformer 训练中研究过\\\"\\n  - \\\"多指标 panel 框架缺失——无人同时追踪 ECE + effective rank + diversity + perplexity 的时序行为并分析其 lead-lag 关系\\\"\\n  - \\\"alpha（synthetic ratio）对 canary 灵敏度的影响未被研究——现有工作只关注 alpha 对最终性能的影响，不关注 canary 的 lead time 如何随 alpha 变化\\\"\\n  - \\\"diversity dose-response 非单调性（α=0.25 比 α=0.50 collapse 更严重）无先例报告——He et al. 2025 从理论上预测 risk landscape 非单调，但无人在 LLM diversity 上实证验证\\\"\\n  - \\\"predictive intervention vs reactive intervention 缺少对比——没有工作量化 early warning 带来的实际干预收益\\\"\\n  - \\\"surplexity 和 spectral alignment 未在 iterative collapse 场景下交叉验证\\\"\\n  - \\\"跨任务 canary 普适性未知——reasoning vs retrieval vs creative tasks 中哪些 canary 更可靠\\\"\\n\\nnovelty_assessment: |\\n  我们项目的核心 novelty 在于：\\n\\n  1. **Panel Granger Causality 框架**：首次将多指标时序数据的因果分析方法应用于 model collapse 早期预警。\\n     现有工作（chen2025scales, gambetta2024surplexity）只是事后观察到某些指标先变化，\\n     但没有人做过系统的 lead-lag 量化分析。\\n\\n  2. **多指标 Canary Panel**：同时追踪 ECE、effective rank、Self-BLEU/distinct-n、\\n     perplexity、entropy、surplexity 等指标，分析其相互关系和各自的 lead time。\\n     现有工作都是单一指标或最多两三个指标的孤立分析。\\n\\n  3. **alpha-dependent Canary Sensitivity**：系统研究 synthetic ratio 如何影响各 canary\\n     指标的灵敏度和 lead time。dohmatob2025strong 证明极小的 alpha 就能触发 collapse，\\n     但没人研究 canary 在不同 alpha 下的行为。\\n\\n  4. **与已有工作的差异**：\\n     - vs chen2025scales: 他们发现 ECE 先退化但没有做因果分析；我们用 Granger causality 量化\\n     - vs gambetta2024surplexity: 他们提出 surplexity 用于检测和缓解 collapse；\\n       我们将其纳入更广的 panel 并研究其 lead-lag 特性\\n     - vs he2025closer: 他们在 diffusion 中用 entropy 作为指标；我们在 LLM 中测试更多指标\\n     - vs qiu2025spectral: 他们的 SA 用于训练 loss explosion 预警；\\n       我们将类似思路迁移到 iterative collapse 场景\\n\\n  5. **潜在风险**：chen2025scales 已经非常接近我们的方向。如果他们扩展到 lead-lag 分析，\\n     我们的 novelty 会被削弱。需要快速推进并在方法论（Granger causality）上建立壁垒。\\n\", \"total_chars\": 32680, \"truncated\": false}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {}}"

```text
1	\section{Related Work}
2	\label{sec:related_work}
3	
4	\paragraph{Model collapse in iterative training.}
5	
6	The phenomenon of iterative training collapse was formalized by \citet{shumailov2024curse}, who showed that recursively replacing real data with model-generated text causes progressive tail erosion across VAEs, GMMs, and language models, and by \citet{alemohammad2024mad}, who demonstrated quality--diversity tradeoffs in self-consuming generative loops for image models.
7	Theoretical analyses have since sharpened these results: \citet{dohmatob2024tale} recast collapse as a change in scaling-law exponents, showing that even small fractions of synthetic data alter effective scaling behavior, while \citet{dohmatob2025strong} proved that collapse can occur even when synthetic data constitutes a vanishingly small fraction of the training set.
8	\citet{hataya2023will} provided early empirical evidence that large-scale generative models degrade future datasets through web-scale contamination, showing that even state-of-the-art models produce training-data artifacts detectable in downstream models, a finding that motivates our focus on contamination-level-dependent analysis.
9	\citet{seddik2024bad} formalized the statistical analysis of training on synthetic data, deriving excess risk bounds that quantify how synthetic fraction and model misspecification jointly govern degradation; their framework predicts regime-dependent behavior consistent with the onset boundary we observe empirically at $\alpha{\approx}0.25$--$0.50$.
10	The role of data accumulation has been a central theme.
11	\citet{dey2024breaking} proved that accumulating data across generations (rather than replacing it) prevents divergence of population risk in GMMs, HMMs, and language models.
12	\citet{kazdan2025collapse} systematically compared replace and accumulate regimes, finding that synthetic data value depends on the absolute quantity of co-present real data.
13	\citet{fu2025selfconsuming} provided generalization bounds for self-consuming loops, establishing that a constant fraction of real data suffices for convergence under accumulation.
14	On the calibration side, \citet{he2025golden} showed that optimal real-data weighting converges to the golden-ratio inverse ($1/\varphi \approx 0.618$) in Gaussian and GLM settings, and that the risk landscape is non-monotone in mixing ratio, a theoretical prediction with implications for regime-dependent canary behavior.
15	Beyond distributional statistics, \citet{guo2024linguistic} documented progressive decline in lexical, syntactic, and semantic diversity across iterative fine-tuning generations, providing the multi-level diversity metrics that serve as downstream targets in our lead-lag analysis.
16	\citet{schaeffer2025position} catalogued eight distinct and sometimes contradictory definitions of ``model collapse'' in the literature, underscoring the importance of explicit operational definitions, a concern our framework addresses by scoping canary prediction to diversity metrics specifically.
17	
18	\paragraph{Canary metrics and collapse monitoring.}
19	
20	Several recent works have proposed monitoring metrics that precede or accompany distributional degradation during iterative training.
21	\citet{du2026geometric} observe that calibration error rises before accuracy degrades in iteratively trained language models, interpreting this through a geometric-regulation lens.
22	\citet{du2025scales} report a similar pattern across model scales and mixing ratios, noting that ECE increases of 75\% appear at 30\% synthetic contamination before accuracy drops become visible.
23	Beyond calibration, SIGMA~\cite{sigma2026spectral} introduces a dual-track spectral framework based on embedding Gram matrices, where divergence between deterministic and stochastic spectral bounds serves as an early collapse diagnostic.
24	\citet{kalinowski2026topology} proposes a topological monitoring approach using persistent homology features to detect multi-scale structural loss in representation space.
25	DCES~\cite{dces2026counter} uses center-edge sampling dynamics as a collapse signal and reports improvements in ECE and entropy preservation.
26	\citet{gambetta2024surplexity} introduces surplexity, a surprise-based metric that simultaneously detects collapse and filters training data, demonstrating that high-surplexity selection matches human-data baselines in maintaining distributional balance.
27	
28	These works collectively demonstrate growing interest in collapse monitoring, yet they share methodological limitations that weaken their lead-lag claims.
29	None apply differencing tests to distinguish genuine temporal precedence from spurious co-trending; none cross-validate findings across multiple statistical methods; and none apply false discovery rate correction when testing multiple metric pairs.
30	Our work differs from the closest predecessor, \citet{du2026geometric}, in four respects:
31	(i)~we compare an entire family of canary metrics rather than a single metric pair;
32	(ii)~we cross-validate each lead-lag relationship across five independent statistical methods, quantifying inter-method sensitivity via Cohen's $\kappa$ and intra-method sensitivity via the Toda--Yamamoto augmentation order $d_{\max}$;
33	(iii)~we map detectability as a function of contamination regime rather than reporting a single aggregate result; and
34	(iv)~we treat method-selection sensitivity itself as a primary finding, showing that it is the dominant source of variation in existing lead-lag conclusions.
35	
36	\paragraph{Training dynamics monitoring.}
37	
38	A broader literature tracks within-training diagnostics such as loss of plasticity~\cite{dohare2024plasticity,lyle2023plasticity}, effective representation rank, and gradient norm statistics as indicators of learning health.
39	These approaches monitor a single training run and detect pathologies (saturated units, rank collapse, unstable gradients) that emerge over optimization steps.
40	Our work differs in a key structural way: we analyze \emph{cross-generation} temporal relationships between aggregate statistics computed after each full training cycle, applying time-series causal methods to a sequence of $T{=}10$ generation-level observations rather than tracking step-level dynamics within a single run.
41	This distinction is methodologically consequential because the short, non-stationary series that arise from generational measurement require entirely different statistical tools (Granger causality, permutation tests, differencing diagnostics) than the long, dense traces available from step-level monitoring.
42	
43	\paragraph{Early-warning signals and critical transitions.}
44	
45	The theoretical foundation for canary-based prediction of system failure originates in ecology and climate science.
46	\citet{scheffer2009early} established that complex systems approaching tipping points exhibit generic early-warning signals (rising autocorrelation and variance due to critical slowing down), independent of system-specific mechanisms.
47	\citet{scheffer2012anticipating} extended this framework by combining empirical warning indicators with network-architecture analysis, arguing that structural and statistical signals together improve anticipatory power.
48	Crucially, \citet{boettiger2012limits} quantified the statistical detection limits of these signals, showing that early-warning power is often negligible in short time series, a constraint directly relevant to iterative training, where the number of generations (typically 5--20) is far smaller than typical ecological datasets.
49	\citet{truong2025entropy} connects entropy collapse to critical transitions, characterizing it as a first-order phase transition with universality classes that determine whether classical early-warning signals retain predictive power.
50	The model collapse literature has not yet imported these methodological tools: existing canary studies neither frame their claims in terms of detection power under finite samples nor test whether observed lead-lag patterns survive the short-series constraints that \citet{boettiger2012limits} identified as fundamental.
51	
52	\paragraph{Lead-lag methodology.}
53	
54	Granger causality~\cite{granger1969investigating} provides the standard econometric framework for testing whether one time series improves prediction of another beyond its own history.
55	\citet{shojaie2022granger} review classical and modern extensions, including VAR-based tests, kernel methods, and neural approaches for nonlinear dependencies \cite{marcinkevics2022neural}.
56	A key practical issue is non-stationarity: standard Granger tests applied to integrated (unit-root) series produce inflated rejection rates, the spurious regression problem formalized by \citet{granger1974spurious}, who demonstrated that regressions between independent random walks yield spuriously significant $t$-statistics, a hazard directly applicable to co-trending metrics in iterative training.
57	The Toda--Yamamoto procedure~\cite{toda1995statistical} addresses this by augmenting the VAR lag order by the integration order $d_{\max}$ and testing only the original lags, yielding valid asymptotic inference regardless of cointegration structure; however, this robustness comes at the cost of reduced degrees of freedom, a trade-off that becomes severe in the short series ($T{\leq}20$) typical of iterative training studies.
58	Despite wide use in economics and neuroscience, these methods are largely absent from the machine learning literature on iterative training.
59	Existing canary studies rely on visual inspection or naive cross-correlation, which cannot distinguish genuine temporal precedence from shared non-stationary trends.
60	Our framework applies five complementary methods (naive cross-correlation, differenced cross-correlation, threshold onset detection, permutation Granger causality, and Toda--Yamamoto Granger causality) and requires multi-method consensus before classifying a lead-lag relationship as robust, directly addressing the method-sensitivity problem that arises when any single test is taken in isolation.
61	Recent work on causal method reliability reinforces this multi-method rationale.
62	\citet{ruiz2026causalaudit} propose Causal-Audit, a pre-screening framework that validates causal assumptions before method selection and achieves 62\% false positive reduction; whereas their approach filters methods \emph{ex ante}, our consensus framework validates conclusions \emph{ex post} across methods already applied.
63	\citet{yi2026causalcompass} benchmark causal discovery robustness under eight misspecification scenarios and confirm that no single method dominates universally, directly supporting the multi-method rationale of our consensus framework.
64	Independently, \citet{sahoo2026calibration} show that calibration metrics are sensitive to training-distribution perturbation beyond the iterative collapse setting, suggesting that ECE-based canary signals may be confounded by non-collapse sources of calibration drift.
65	\citet{falahati2026curated} prove that pluralistic curation of synthetic data can prevent iterative collapse entirely, implying that canary detection is most relevant in uncontrolled data pipelines where such curation is absent.
66	
67	In summary, the model collapse literature has established the phenomenon and proposed monitoring metrics, while the econometric literature provides the statistical tools for temporal precedence testing.
68	However, no prior work bridges these two bodies of literature with the methodological rigor that short, non-stationary time series demand: systematic multi-method cross-validation, differencing diagnostics, false discovery correction, and regime-dependent calibration.
69	Our consensus framework fills this gap.
```

1. Primary Request and Intent:
   The Paper Agent is executing sequential Director-issued Patch instructions for an EMNLP 2026 paper on "Distributional Canary Collapse" (project: distrib_canary_collapse). This session covered 8 Director instructions:
   1. Complete generalization experiment framework (§4.5 + limitations + contribution placeholder)
   2. Discussion + Limitations deep polish + em-dash cleanup (6 sub-tasks)
   3. Full em-dash cleanup across remaining 7 .tex files (~41 fixes)
   4. Reviewer audit fix (2 items: L27 number error + unit-test analogy removal)

   6. Integrate 2 new visualizations (Shapley figure + single-method-errors table) + fix Shapley numbers
   7. URGENT CORRECTION: Shapley v3 number update across all files
   8. **[CURRENT, NOT YET STARTED]** Create literature comparison table in Related Work

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **Granger precedence** (NOT causation): temporal predictive improvement in time-series
   - **Consensus framework**: K≥4/5 methods, S≥3/4 seeds; unique operating point where FPR=0% and FNR=0%
   - **Shapley value decomposition**: exact 32-coalition enumeration, K=max(1,|S|−1) threshold
   - **Shapley v3 values**: Naive XCorr FPR=+0.348, PermG FPR=−0.194, TY FNR=−0.158 vs −0.210 for others
   - **Onset boundary**: α≈0.25–0.50 for causal detectability
   - **α=0.50 three-seed results**: s42=0/8, s43=6/8, s44=3/8 PermG (majority-signal, transitional)
   - **ECE as α=0.50 canary**: more sensitive than token entropy at boundary due to bin-level decomposition
   - **Paper-writing-norms**: no em-dashes (---), no -ing clause endings, no AI style markers
   - **Communication protocol**: Paper Agent uses `paper_agent_post_to_director`, NOT `main_agent_post_to_director`; system reminders about the latter are false positives to ignore

3. Files and Code Sections:

   - **`docs/paper/method.tex`** (heavily modified)
     - Shapley paragraph (L72-73): Updated three times. Final v3 values: Naive XCorr FPR=+0.348, PermG FPR=−0.194, TY FNR=−0.158 vs −0.210
     - New Figure block (L75-80): `\label{fig:shapley}`, caption with 32 coalitions, K=max(1,|S|−1), FPR/FNR data sources
     - 8 em-dashes cleaned (parentheses, commas, colons)
     - K=4/5 tipping point paragraph added (replaced old "FPR/FNR calibration symmetry")
     - L27: Fixed "2/6 reference pairs" → "3/6 reference pairs" for FNR=50% consistency

   - **`docs/paper/discussion.tex`** (heavily modified)
     - L23-28: New "Why K=4/5 is the consensus tipping point" paragraph (replaced calibration symmetry)
     - L27: "3/6 reference pairs (FNR=50%)" — corrected from 2/6
     - After L53: ECE boundary sensitivity explanation (2 sentences on bin-level decomposition vs global average)
     - After L60: "Why this matters despite the paradox" bridge paragraph (4 sentences: α unknown/increasing, methodology independence, regime map actionable)
     - L62-65: "Broader implications" rewritten — removed "unit test"/"quality gate", replaced with "continuous monitoring signal" scoped to α≥0.75, references paradox + no-intervention limitation

        "content": "\\section{Introduction}\n\\label{sec:introduction}\n\nPractitioners monitoring iterative training pipelines face a diagnostic blind spot: the metrics most commonly used to detect distributional degradation can yield false positive rates of 62.5--100\\% when applied without proper statistical controls, yet no established protocol exists for distinguishing genuine early-warning signals from artifacts of shared non-stationary trends.\nThis blind spot is consequential because iterative training collapse, where models are unwittingly trained on output from earlier generations, is increasingly likely as machine-generated text proliferates on the web~\\cite{shumailov2024curse,alemohammad2024mad}.\nTail modes vanish, lexical diversity narrows, and calibration deteriorates across generations.\nThese failure modes motivate the search for cheap-to-compute \\emph{canary metrics} that provide early warning, analogous to early-warning signals for critical transitions in ecological and climate systems~\\cite{scheffer2009early,scheffer2012anticipating}.\n\nSeveral recent studies have pursued this direction: \\citet{du2026geometric} informally observe that expected calibration error rises before accuracy degrades; SIGMA~\\cite{sigma2026spectral} proposes spectral indicators of distributional shift; and DCES~\\cite{dces2026counter} uses center-edge sampling dynamics as a collapse signal.\nYet the statistical methodology underlying these lead-lag claims (whether a canary metric genuinely \\emph{precedes} downstream degradation, or merely correlates with it under shared non-stationary trends) has received little scrutiny.\n\nWe identify several methodological deficiencies that pervade existing lead-lag analyses of iterative training collapse:\n(1)~no differencing tests to rule out spurious regression arising from shared non-stationary trends, a well-known hazard when correlating co-trending time series;\n(2)~no multi-method cross-validation; conclusions rest on a single statistical method whose implicit assumptions about stationarity and directionality are rarely examined;\n(3)~no multiple comparison correction (e.g., Benjamini--Hochberg FDR control), increasing the risk of false discoveries when testing many metric pairs simultaneously; and\n(4)~no regime-dependent analysis, despite the possibility that the same statistical test yields opposite conclusions at different contamination levels.\nThe closest predecessor is \\citet{du2026geometric}, who observe that calibration error rises before accuracy degrades in iteratively trained language models.\nTheir analysis, however, is limited to a single metric pair, spans only 1--3 generations, and reports informal visual comparisons without statistical tests, leaving open the question of whether the observed lead is genuine or an artifact of shared trends.\nThe early-warning framework of \\citet{scheffer2009early,scheffer2012anticipating}, widely adopted in ecology for anticipating tipping points, provides the theoretical motivation for canary-based prediction, but has not been systematically applied to iterative training collapse with the methodological rigor that short, noisy time series demand~\\cite{boettiger2012limits}.\n\nWe address these gaps through a systematic case study of canary prediction of diversity collapse, the progressive loss of lexical diversity in model-generated text across training generations.\nUsing Pythia-410M across five contamination levels ($\\alpha \\in \\{0.00, 0.25, 0.50, 0.75, 1.00\\}$) and up to four random seeds, we expose three methodological pitfalls that undermine naive lead-lag analysis:\n\\begin{enumerate}\n    \\item \\textbf{Method sensitivity.} Applied to the same data (16 candidate canary$\\to$downstream pairs, including candidates later excluded; \\S\\ref{sec:case_study}), five lead-lag detection methods yield FDR significance rates ranging from 100\\% (naive cross-correlation) to 25\\% (Toda--Yamamoto Granger causality), with inter-method agreement of Cohen's $\\kappa{=}0.185$ (poor agreement).\n    \\item \\textbf{Spurious regression.} Canary$\\to$perplexity associations appear near-universal in undifferenced analysis but vanish entirely after first-differencing ($F{\\approx}0$, $p{=}0.99$), revealing them as artifacts of co-trending rather than genuine temporal precedence.\n    \\item \\textbf{Regime dependence.} Permutation Granger tests find 24/24 canary$\\to$diversity pairs FDR-significant at full synthetic replacement ($\\alpha{=}1.0$) but 0/24 at $\\alpha{\\leq}0.25$ (three seeds), with seed-dependent onset at $\\alpha{=}0.50$ (0/8--6/8, three seeds), demonstrating that detectability depends sharply on both contamination level and training stochasticity.\n\\end{enumerate}\nTo move beyond single-method analysis, we propose a multi-method consensus framework with empirically calibrated thresholds, requiring agreement across multiple independent statistical methods and random seeds before classifying a lead-lag relationship as robust.\n\nThe framework's utility is demonstrated by its calibration on built-in experimental controls.\nNaive cross-correlation identifies 100\\% of canary--collapse metric pairs as FDR-significant; the consensus pipeline (requiring ${\\geq}4/5$ methods and ${\\geq}3/4$ seeds) retains only those pairs meeting effect-size criteria calibrated against the null control.\nOn reference extremes, the framework achieves a false positive rate of 0\\% at the $\\alpha{=}0.0$ null control (0/24 pairs, three seeds) (where individual methods reach up to 100\\% FPR) and a false negative rate of 0\\% against six effect-size reference pairs at $\\alpha{=}1.0$.\nApplying the framework, we identify token entropy as a one-generation-ahead canary for diversity collapse at $\\alpha{=}1.0$: the full consensus pipeline confirms directional asymmetry with cross-correlation $|r|{>}0.99$, 24/24 forward-direction pairs FDR-significant, and 0/24 in the reverse direction.\nThree-seed replication at $\\alpha{=}0.75$ extends this finding (19/24 forward, 0/24 reverse via permutation Granger), though method-level consensus remains transitional (Tier~2--3).\nThe onset boundary of causal detectability lies at $\\alpha{\\approx}0.25$--$0.50$, below which all seeds agree on null.\nWe note that Granger precedence establishes temporal predictive improvement, not true causality~\\cite{shojaie2022granger}.\n\nOur contributions are:\n\\begin{enumerate}\n    \\item \\textbf{Systematic exposure of three methodological pitfalls} (method sensitivity, spurious regression, regime dependence) in lead-lag analysis of iterative training collapse.\n    Method choice alone inflates spurious-signal rates to at least 62.5\\%; first-differencing eliminates canary$\\to$perplexity associations entirely; and the same Granger test shifts from 24/24 FDR-significant pairs to 0/8 across contamination levels.\n    These results demonstrate that single-method, single-regime analyses, the current norm in this literature, are unreliable.\n\n    \\item \\textbf{A multi-method consensus framework} that functions as a diagnostic filter rather than a majority vote: the $K{\\geq}4/5$ threshold is the unique operating point where both FPR and FNR reach 0\\% on reference extremes ($K{=}3$: FPR${=}$37.5\\%; $K{=}5$: FNR${=}$50\\%).\n    Shapley-value decomposition (exact 32-coalition enumeration) reveals that naive cross-correlation is the sole FPR driver (Shapley(FPR)${=}{+}0.348$) while permutation Granger is the strongest FPR suppressor (Shapley(FPR)${=}{-}0.194$); these diagnostics provide principled guidance for method panel selection.\n    The framework achieves FNR${=}$0\\% on effect-size reference pairs (4-method panel, permutation Granger power${=}1.0$) with four-tier practical guidance.\n\n    \\item \\textbf{Identification of token entropy as a robust one-generation-ahead canary} for diversity collapse at $\\alpha{=}1.0$, confirmed by 4 independent methods, 4 seeds, and cross-architecture replication on GPT-2-medium, with complete directional asymmetry (24/24 forward, 0/24 reverse); directional asymmetry extends to $\\alpha{=}0.75$ across 3~seeds (19/24 forward, 0/24 reverse) in a transitional consensus regime (Tier~2--3). The claim is scoped to diversity metrics (distinct-$n$), not model collapse broadly.\n\n    \\item \\textbf{Cross-architecture generalization} (\\S\\ref{sec:generalization}): validation on GPT-2-medium (345M parameters) confirms that the entropy-leads-diversity pattern, the consensus framework's operating thresholds, and the detectability boundary at $\\alpha{\\approx}0.25$--$0.50$ all transfer across decoder-only transformer families, with all 6 canary$\\to$diversity pairs reaching Tier~3 consensus at $\\alpha{=}1.0$ and 0/6 in the reverse direction.\n\\end{enumerate}\n\nThe remainder of this paper is organized as follows.\n\\S\\ref{sec:related_work} surveys related work on model collapse, early-warning signals, and lead-lag methodology.\n\\S\\ref{sec:background} establishes the formal setup, notation, and the five detection methods used throughout.\n\\S\\ref{sec:method} distills the three methodological pitfalls into a unified diagnostic framework, presents the multi-method consensus pipeline, and reports its empirical calibration.\n\\S\\ref{sec:experiments} provides full experimental results: method sensitivity analysis (\\S\\ref{sec:method_sensitivity}), regime-dependent detectability (\\S\\ref{sec:regime}), a case study of canary prediction of diversity collapse (\\S\\ref{sec:case_study}), consensus framework validation (\\S\\ref{sec:consensus_validation}), and cross-architecture generalization (\\S\\ref{sec:generalization}).\n\\S\\ref{sec:discussion} discusses implications (including the practical utility paradox, the token entropy--perplexity distinction, and NLP-specific constraints on lead-lag analysis) and outlines directions for future work.\n\\S\\ref{sec:conclusion} concludes, followed by a dedicated Limitations section (\\S\\ref{sec:limitations}).",

        "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0514_4/projects/distrib_canary_collapse/docs/paper/method.tex",
        "content": "\\section{Method}\n\\label{sec:method}\n\nThe experiments in \\S\\ref{sec:method_sensitivity}--\\ref{sec:consensus_validation} reveal that lead-lag conclusions in iterative training collapse are fragile along multiple axes.\nThis section distills three methodological pitfalls (\\S\\ref{sec:pitfalls}), proposes a multi-method consensus framework to address them (\\S\\ref{sec:consensus}), and calibrates the framework's error rates using built-in experimental controls (\\S\\ref{sec:calibration}).\n\n\n\\subsection{Methodological Pitfalls in Lead-Lag Analysis}\n\\label{sec:pitfalls}\n\nWe identify three pitfalls that, individually or in combination, can produce incorrect conclusions about temporal precedence in iterative training systems.\nEach is documented below with representative data from our experiments; full results appear in \\S\\ref{sec:experiments}.\n\n\\paragraph{Pitfall 1: Method sensitivity.}\nApplied to the same data ($\\alpha{=}1.0$, 4~seeds, 16 canary$\\to$downstream pairs: 4 candidate canaries $\\times$ 4 downstream metrics, including candidates later excluded per \\S\\ref{sec:case_study}), five lead-lag detection methods yield FDR significance rates ranging from 100\\% (naive cross-correlation) to 25\\% (Toda--Yamamoto Granger causality), with inter-method agreement of Cohen's $\\kappa{=}0.185$, indicating poor agreement (\\S\\ref{sec:method_sensitivity}).\nThe spurious-signal rate (the fraction of pairs flagged by the most liberal method but not confirmed by the most conservative) is 75\\% across all five methods and 62.5\\% when excluding Toda--Yamamoto, whose low statistical power at $T{=}10$ inflates this rate.\nThis pitfall is dangerous because method choice alone can determine whether a lead-lag relationship is deemed present or absent.\nIn a literature where single-method analyses are the norm, the implicit assumption that method choice does not matter is demonstrably false.\n\n\\paragraph{Pitfall 2: Spurious regression under shared trends.}\nA single preprocessing decision, first-differencing to remove shared non-stationary trends~\\cite{granger1974spurious}, can eliminate an entire analysis channel.\nAt $\\alpha{=}1.0$, canary$\\to$perplexity pairs appear near-universally significant in undifferenced analysis ($F{=}16$--$36$) but yield $F{\\approx}0$, $p{=}0.99$ after differencing (Figure~\\ref{fig:differencing_dmax}a; \\S\\ref{sec:method_sensitivity}).\nIn contrast, canary$\\to$diversity pairs retain unanimous FDR significance after differencing (24/24$\\to$24/24).\nWithout the differencing diagnostic, an analyst would conclude that canaries predict perplexity, a conclusion entirely driven by co-trending rather than temporal precedence.\nThe danger lies in the selective nature of this confound: only by applying the diagnostic can one determine which channels are genuine and which are artifacts of shared non-stationarity.\n\n\\paragraph{Pitfall 3: Regime dependence.}\nEven after fixing the statistical method, the contamination level~$\\alpha$ determines whether a lead-lag relationship is detectable.\nPermutation Granger tests (first-differenced, BH FDR-corrected at $q{=}0.05$) find 6/8 pairs FDR-significant at $\\alpha{=}1.0$ but 0/24 at $\\alpha{\\leq}0.25$ (three seeds), with seed-dependent results (0/8--6/8) at $\\alpha{=}0.50$ (\\S\\ref{sec:regime}).\nThe $\\alpha{=}0.50$ regime is particularly dangerous: for seed~42, correlation-based methods unanimously find significance while causal methods find non-significance (Figure~\\ref{fig:consensus_heatmap}); for other seeds, partial Granger significance emerges (3/8 and 6/8), illustrating seed-dependent divergence at this boundary and creating the maximum risk of spurious conclusions when only a single seed is analyzed.\nThis regime dependence is consistent with theoretical expectations about signal-to-noise limits in short ecological time series \\cite{boettiger2012limits}, and suggests that detectability boundaries should be explicitly mapped rather than assumed.\n\n\n\\subsection{Multi-Method Consensus Framework}\n\\label{sec:consensus}\n\nThe pitfalls above motivate a consensus approach inspired by multi-method validation practices in early-warning signal detection \\cite{scheffer2009early}: rather than relying on any single method, we require agreement across multiple independent methods and random seeds before classifying a lead-lag relationship as robust.\n\n\\paragraph{Formal definition.}\nGiven $M$ lead-lag detection methods $\\{m_1, \\ldots, m_M\\}$ and $N$ independent random seeds, a canary$\\to$downstream pair $(X, Y)$ is classified as \\textsc{robust} if and only if:\n\\begin{enumerate}\n    \\item At least $K$ of $M$ methods judge the pair FDR-significant, where each method independently applies Benjamini--Hochberg correction at $q{=}0.05$; and\n    \\item At least $S$ of $N$ seeds replicate this conclusion.\n\\end{enumerate}\nThe thresholds $(K, S)$ control the trade-off between false positive and false negative rates.\nMethods with different inductive biases (correlation-based methods that detect any temporal association versus Granger-type methods that test directional predictive improvement \\cite{granger1969investigating}) provide complementary evidence.\nTheir convergence on a given pair constitutes stronger evidence than agreement among methods sharing the same statistical assumptions.\nWe note that Granger precedence establishes temporal predictive improvement, not true causality \\cite{shojaie2022granger}; our framework inherits this limitation.\n\n\\paragraph{Sequential filtering pipeline.}\nIn practice, the $K$-of-$M$ voting operates over all five methods applied independently to each pair; the sequential pipeline below describes the order in which results are examined, enabling early termination and interpretable diagnostics at each stage (Figure~\\ref{fig:consensus_workflow}):\n\\begin{enumerate}\n    \\item \\textbf{Naive correlation scan.} Cross-correlation at maximum lag~5 with BH correction serves as a permissive baseline; all pairs with any temporal association pass.\n    \\item \\textbf{First-differencing diagnostic.} Cross-correlation is recomputed on first-differenced series, testing whether temporal associations survive removal of shared linear trends. Pairs that lose significance are flagged as potential spurious regressions.\n    \\item \\textbf{Permutation Granger causality.} Permutation-based Granger causality tests \\cite{shojaie2022granger} on first-differenced series (5{,}000 permutations, BH correction at $q{=}0.05$) assess directional predictive improvement: whether past values of $X$ improve prediction of $Y$ beyond $Y$'s own history.\n    \\item \\textbf{Multi-seed replication.} The conclusion must hold across $S$ of $N$ independent training seeds, filtering seed-specific artifacts.\n\\end{enumerate}\nAt $\\alpha{=}1.0$, this pipeline progressively filters: 16 pairs pass Stage~1, 14 survive Stage~2 (differencing removes 2 marginal canary$\\to$perplexity pairs), 6 survive Stage~3 (Granger testing eliminates remaining canary$\\to$perplexity pairs and failed canary candidates), and all~6 survive Stage~4 (multi-seed replication confirms all Granger-significant pairs).\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\linewidth]{figures/consensus_workflow.pdf}\n\\caption{Sequential filtering pipeline of the multi-method consensus framework. Starting from all candidate canary$\\to$downstream pairs, the pipeline applies four stages: (1)~naive cross-correlation scan (permissive baseline), (2)~first-differencing diagnostic (removes shared-trend artifacts), (3)~permutation Granger causality (tests directional predictive improvement), and (4)~multi-seed replication (filters seed-specific artifacts). At $\\alpha{=}1.0$ (Pythia-410M), the pipeline reduces 16 candidate pairs to 6 robust pairs: 16$\\to$14$\\to$6$\\to$6. The $K$-of-$M$ consensus vote (requiring $K{\\geq}4/5$ methods) operates in parallel across all five methods; the sequential ordering provides interpretable diagnostics at each stage.}\n\\label{fig:consensus_workflow}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\paragraph{Practical guidance.}\nThe consensus thresholds $(K, S)$ should be chosen to match the analyst's tolerance for false positives versus false negatives.\nTable~\\ref{tab:practical_guidance} provides four recommended tiers, calibrated against our experimental controls (\\S\\ref{sec:calibration}).\nTier~3 ($K{\\geq}4/5$, $S{\\geq}3/4$) achieves the best trade-off in our setting: zero false positives while recovering all six reference pairs.\nMethod subset ablation confirms that this threshold is uniquely optimal: at $K{=}3/5$, the null-control FPR is 37.5\\% (unacceptable); at $K{=}4/5$, FPR drops to 0\\% while retaining 6/6 reference-pair sensitivity; at $K{=}5/5$, FPR remains 0\\% but sensitivity falls to 3/6.\nShapley value analysis (exact enumeration of all 32 coalitions; Figure~\\ref{fig:shapley}) identifies naive cross-correlation as the sole positive contributor to FPR (Shapley(FPR)${=}{+}0.348$) and permutation Granger as the strongest FPR suppressor (Shapley(FPR)${=}{-}0.194$), the only method combining 0\\% null-control FPR with high sensitivity.\nToda--Yamamoto is the weakest contributor to sensitivity (Shapley(FNR)${=}{-}0.158$ vs.\\ ${-}0.210$ for all other methods); we retain it as insurance against unknown integration order, but note its severe power limitations at $T{\\leq}10$.\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\linewidth]{figures/shapley_method_contribution.pdf}\n\\caption{Shapley value decomposition of method contributions to consensus FPR and FNR (exact enumeration of all $2^5{=}32$ coalitions, $K{=}\\max(1,|S|{-}1)$). FPR is computed at $\\alpha{=}0.0$ (3~seeds $\\times$ 8~pairs${=}$24 observations); FNR at $\\alpha{=}1.0$ (4~seeds, 6 reference pairs). Naive cross-correlation is the only positive FPR contributor (${+}0.348$); permutation Granger is the strongest FPR suppressor (${-}0.194$). Toda--Yamamoto is the weakest sensitivity contributor (${-}0.158$ vs.\\ ${-}0.210$ for all other methods), consistent with its low power at $T{=}10$.}\n\\label{fig:shapley}\n\\end{figure}\nTier~4 requires unanimity across methods and seeds, which incurs a 50\\% false negative rate because Toda--Yamamoto produces Type~II errors at $T{=}10$ due to insufficient residual degrees of freedom (\\S\\ref{sec:consensus_validation}).\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Practical guidance tiers for lead-lag consensus analysis. Tier~3 ($K{\\geq}4/5$, $S{\\geq}3/4$) is uniquely optimal: it achieves FPR${=}$0\\% while retaining all 6 reference pairs (FNR${=}$0\\%). FPR is calibrated at $\\alpha{=}0.0$ (null control, $M{=}5$ methods, $N{=}3$ seeds, Pythia-410M). FNR is calibrated at $\\alpha{=}1.0$ against 6 effect-size reference pairs using a 4-method panel (excluding differenced cross-correlation); $N{=}4$ seeds (\\S\\ref{sec:calibration}).}\n\\label{tab:practical_guidance}\n\\begin{tabular}{llcccc}\n\\toprule\nTier & Use Case & $K/M$ & $S/N$ & FPR & FNR \\\\\n\\midrule\n1 & Exploratory     & ${\\geq}1/5$ & ${\\geq}1/N$ & 100\\%  & 0\\%    \\\\\n2 & Screening       & ${\\geq}3/5$ & ${\\geq}2/N$ & 37.5\\% & 0\\%    \\\\\n3 & Confirmatory    & ${\\geq}4/5$ & ${\\geq}3/N$ & 0\\%    & 0\\%    \\\\\n4 & Conservative    & $5/5$       & $N/N$        & 0\\%    & 50\\% \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\n\\subsection{Empirical Calibration}\n\\label{sec:calibration}\n\nWe calibrate the framework using two built-in experimental controls.\nFull numerical results are reported in Table~\\ref{tab:fpr_fnr}.\n\n\\paragraph{False positive rate.}\nThe $\\alpha{=}0.0$ condition serves as a null control: no synthetic data is injected, so any detected lead-lag association is a false positive.\nAt $K{=}1$ (any single method suffices), FPR${=}$100\\%: naive cross-correlation alone flags all pairs.\nAt $K{=}2$, FPR drops to 75\\%; at $K{=}3$, to 37.5\\%; and at $K{\\geq}4$, FPR${=}$0\\%.\nThe consensus framework at Tier~3 matches the false positive performance of the best individual methods (permutation Granger and Toda--Yamamoto, both 0\\%) while providing robustness against miscalibration of any single method.\n\n\\paragraph{False negative rate.}\nAt $\\alpha{=}1.0$ with 4~seeds, we define six \\emph{effect-size reference pairs} using criteria independent of the consensus methods:\n\\begin{enumerate}\n    \\item[(a)] The canary$\\to$downstream drift ratio at $\\alpha{=}1.0$ must exceed $3\\times$ the $\\alpha{=}0.0$ baseline drift (canary ratios: entropy $11.93\\times$, ECE $73.12\\times$, both far above threshold); and\n    \\item[(b)] Canary onset must strictly precede downstream onset by at least one generation (canary onset: generation~1; diversity onset: generations~3--9).\n\\end{enumerate}\nThese criteria yield token entropy and ECE each predicting distinct-1, distinct-2, and distinct-3~\\cite{li2016diversity}.\nThe perplexity channel is excluded because its onset (generation~1) coincides with canary onset, consistent with, but not derived from, the differencing diagnostic finding that canary$\\to$perplexity associations are spurious (\\S\\ref{sec:method_sensitivity}).\nThe effect-size threshold is robust: at $2\\times$, $3\\times$, and $5\\times$ the baseline drift, the reference set remains identical (6~pairs), because canary ratios ($11.93\\times$ and $73.12\\times$) far exceed all thresholds.\n\nFNR is calibrated using a 4-method panel excluding differenced cross-correlation, which shares statistical properties with detrending-based criteria and could introduce circularity.\nAt $K{\\geq}3/4$, $S{\\geq}3/4$, all six reference pairs are recovered (FNR${=}$0\\%).\nAt $K{=}4/4$, $S{=}4/4$, FNR rises to 50\\% because Toda--Yamamoto produces Type~II errors at $T{=}10$ due to insufficient residual degrees of freedom (\\S\\ref{sec:consensus_validation}).\n\n\\paragraph{Symmetric calibration.}\nBoth FPR and FNR are validated on the joint method${\\times}$seed dimension: FPR across 3~seeds at $\\alpha{=}0.0$ (0/24 pairs at $K{\\geq}4/5$) and FNR across 4~seeds at $\\alpha{=}1.0$ (6/6 reference pairs recovered at $K{\\geq}3/4$, $S{\\geq}3/4$).\nCross-seed FPR variance is negligible: all three null-control seeds yield 0/8 robust pairs at the consensus threshold, confirming seed-independent performance.",
        "numLines": 129,
        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 129
      }
    },
    "displayPath": "../../../docs/paper/method.tex"
  },

        "content": "# Leave-One-Out Method Contribution Analysis\n\n## Setup\n- Consensus rule: K>=3 out of 5 methods (full), K>=3 out of 4 (LOO)\n- FPR computed at α=0.0 (null, 2 seeds × 8 pairs = 16 obs)\n- FNR computed at α=1.0 (4 seeds × 8 pairs, cross-seed aggregated = 8 obs)\n- Baseline: FPR=25.0%, FNR=0.0%\n\n## Results\n\n| Method           | ΔFPR     | ΔFNR     | Interpretation                        |\n|-----------------|----------|----------|---------------------------------------|\n| Naive XCorr     | -25.0%   | +25.0%   | Major FP driver, essential for sensitivity |\n| Diff XCorr      | -25.0%   | +25.0%   | Major FP driver, essential for sensitivity |\n| Threshold Onset | -6.2%    | +25.0%   | Minor FP contributor, essential for sensitivity |\n| Perm Granger    | -18.8%   | 0.0%     | Pure FP filter, no sensitivity cost   |\n| TY Granger      | 0.0%     | 0.0%     | Zero marginal contribution            |\n\n## Key Findings\n1. **Naive XCorr and Diff XCorr** are \"double-edged\": they drive both correct detections and false positives equally\n2. **Threshold Onset** is primarily a sensitivity contributor with minimal FPR contribution (-6.2%)\n3. **Perm Granger** acts as a pure false-positive filter: removing it reduces FPR by 18.8% without affecting sensitivity\n4. **TY Granger** has zero marginal effect on consensus decisions (redundant with other methods)\n5. The three correlation-based methods (naive, diff, threshold) are all individually essential for FNR=0% at α=1.0\n\n## Figure Location\n- Server: `docs/paper/figures/shapley_method_contribution.pdf` (and .png)\n- Data source: `results/method_ablation_phase2_panel.json` + individual `5method_leadlag.json` files\n",
